Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.

That sounds a little stronger than what I mean.\n\nI have had this feeling—maybe I’m being silly—but I get convinced sometimes that certain customers are coming in to sort of check on me, like to see if I’m going to miss payments on the new showroom. They’ll ask a lot of questions about it, and my husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right. But in the moment it feels like they’re asking for a reason. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people are talking about you or that their actions are directed at you, even if they don’t say it outright?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I know it might sound odd. But with some customers, when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just making conversation — like maybe they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to believe that, but in the moment it really feels directed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are acting in a certain way toward you, like they’re watching you or trying to send you a message, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but sometimes with customers, especially when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just being polite.\n\nLike… I’ll get convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or something, because they keep asking questions. My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.\n\nI know it sounds odd, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but I’ve gotten convinced sometimes that they’re coming in to check whether I’m going to miss payments, because they ask so many questions. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. I mean, not really with the news or anything like that. It’s more when people are right there in front of me.\n\nLike I said, with some customers asking so many questions about the new showroom, I start to feel like there’s something behind it… like they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if things are going badly. I know that probably sounds strange, and my husband tells me they’re just being friendly or curious. I try to believe him, I really do. But in the moment, it feels connected to me in that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on the street, a comment from a stranger, or even something in the environment like a noise or a sign, was meant to send you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like signs or noises exactly… not that I can think of. It’s more with people, especially customers.\n\nI know it probably sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I can get this feeling that they’re really checking on me—like whether I’m going to miss payments or if things aren’t going well. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. They’ll ask a lot of questions about it, and my husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right. But in the moment it feels like they’re asking for a reason. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people are talking about you or that their actions are directed at you, even if they don’t say it outright?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I know it might sound odd. But with some customers, when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just making conversation — like maybe they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to believe that, but in the moment it really feels directed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are acting in a certain way toward you, like they’re watching you or trying to send you a message, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but sometimes with customers, especially when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just being polite.\n\nLike… I’ll get convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or something, because they keep asking questions. My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.\n\nI know it sounds odd, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but I’ve gotten convinced sometimes that they’re coming in to check whether I’m going to miss payments, because they ask so many questions. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. I mean, not really with the news or anything like that. It’s more when people are right there in front of me.\n\nLike I said, with some customers asking so many questions about the new showroom, I start to feel like there’s something behind it… like they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if things are going badly. I know that probably sounds strange, and my husband tells me they’re just being friendly or curious. I try to believe him, I really do. But in the moment, it feels connected to me in that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on the street, a comment from a stranger, or even something in the environment like a noise or a sign, was meant to send you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like signs or noises exactly… not that I can think of. It’s more with people, especially customers.\n\nI know it probably sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I can get this feeling that they’re really checking on me—like whether I’m going to miss payments or if things aren’t going well. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced by something or someone outside of yourself, like your mind isn’t fully your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like my thoughts are being controlled, exactly. That sounds stronger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I can get really convinced about something, even when my husband tells me I’m probably taking it the wrong way. Like with customers asking so many questions about the new showroom… I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. I know that may sound odd, and I don’t want to overstate it, but in the moment it feels very real to me. Is that along the lines of what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like at work, someone asks when a ring repair will be done, and I start with that, but then I’m suddenly talking about invoices, or garden soil, or mortgage papers. I can tell from their face that I’m not making sense the way I meant to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… thoughts being put in or taken out, no. That sounds a little stronger than what I mean.\n\nI have had this feeling—maybe I’m being silly—but I get convinced sometimes that certain customers are coming in to sort of check on me, like to see if I’m going to miss payments on the new showroom. They’ll ask a lot of questions about it, and my husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right. But in the moment it feels like they’re asking for a reason. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people are talking about you or that their actions are directed at you, even if they don’t say it outright?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I know it might sound odd. But with some customers, when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just making conversation — like maybe they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to believe that, but in the moment it really feels directed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are acting in a certain way toward you, like they’re watching you or trying to send you a message, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but sometimes with customers, especially when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just being polite.\n\nLike… I’ll get convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or something, because they keep asking questions. My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.\n\nI know it sounds odd, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but I’ve gotten convinced sometimes that they’re coming in to check whether I’m going to miss payments, because they ask so many questions. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. I mean, not really with the news or anything like that. It’s more when people are right there in front of me.\n\nLike I said, with some customers asking so many questions about the new showroom, I start to feel like there’s something behind it… like they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if things are going badly. I know that probably sounds strange, and my husband tells me they’re just being friendly or curious. I try to believe him, I really do. But in the moment, it feels connected to me in that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on the street, a comment from a stranger, or even something in the environment like a noise or a sign, was meant to send you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like signs or noises exactly… not that I can think of. It’s more with people, especially customers.\n\nI know it probably sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I can get this feeling that they’re really checking on me—like whether I’m going to miss payments or if things aren’t going well. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced by something or someone outside of yourself, like your mind isn’t fully your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like my thoughts are being controlled, exactly. That sounds stronger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I can get really convinced about something, even when my husband tells me I’m probably taking it the wrong way. Like with customers asking so many questions about the new showroom… I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. I know that may sound odd, and I don’t want to overstate it, but in the moment it feels very real to me. Is that along the lines of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not your own, like someone else can hear them or that they are being put into your mind?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never felt like someone could hear my thoughts, or like thoughts were being put into my head.\n\nI mean, I do get worked up about what people might mean sometimes, but my thoughts still feel like mine. I don’t want to answer it wrong, but for that specific thing, I don’t think that’s happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

I thought maybe I should ask someone about it.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to explain something, do your thoughts feel disorganized or jumbled inside your head, making it hard to stay on track?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Like at work, someone asks when a ring repair will be done, and I start with that, but then I’m suddenly talking about invoices, or garden soil, or mortgage papers. I can tell from their face that I’m not making sense the way I meant to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… thoughts being put in or taken out, no. That sounds a little stronger than what I mean.\n\nI have had this feeling—maybe I’m being silly—but I get convinced sometimes that certain customers are coming in to sort of check on me, like to see if I’m going to miss payments on the new showroom. They’ll ask a lot of questions about it, and my husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right. But in the moment it feels like they’re asking for a reason. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people are talking about you or that their actions are directed at you, even if they don’t say it outright?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I know it might sound odd. But with some customers, when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just making conversation — like maybe they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to believe that, but in the moment it really feels directed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are acting in a certain way toward you, like they’re watching you or trying to send you a message, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but sometimes with customers, especially when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just being polite.\n\nLike… I’ll get convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or something, because they keep asking questions. My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.\n\nI know it sounds odd, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but I’ve gotten convinced sometimes that they’re coming in to check whether I’m going to miss payments, because they ask so many questions. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. I mean, not really with the news or anything like that. It’s more when people are right there in front of me.\n\nLike I said, with some customers asking so many questions about the new showroom, I start to feel like there’s something behind it… like they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if things are going badly. I know that probably sounds strange, and my husband tells me they’re just being friendly or curious. I try to believe him, I really do. But in the moment, it feels connected to me in that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on the street, a comment from a stranger, or even something in the environment like a noise or a sign, was meant to send you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like signs or noises exactly… not that I can think of. It’s more with people, especially customers.\n\nI know it probably sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I can get this feeling that they’re really checking on me—like whether I’m going to miss payments or if things aren’t going well. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced by something or someone outside of yourself, like your mind isn’t fully your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like my thoughts are being controlled, exactly. That sounds stronger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I can get really convinced about something, even when my husband tells me I’m probably taking it the wrong way. Like with customers asking so many questions about the new showroom… I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. I know that may sound odd, and I don’t want to overstate it, but in the moment it feels very real to me. Is that along the lines of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not your own, like someone else can hear them or that they are being put into your mind?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never felt like someone could hear my thoughts, or like thoughts were being put into my head.\n\nI mean, I do get worked up about what people might mean sometimes, but my thoughts still feel like mine. I don’t want to answer it wrong, but for that specific thing, I don’t think that’s happened.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about customers checking on you or sending you messages felt completely true to you, even when others, like your husband, have given you reasons to doubt them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess in those moments it does feel true to me. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, because I know my husband may be right that they’re just being friendly, but when customers keep asking about the new showroom, I can get really convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong.\n\nAfterward I can see maybe I read too much into it, but at the time it feels hard to shake. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yes… that’s probably fair to say. I know my husband’s explanation makes sense, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but even knowing that, I still get this feeling I can’t quite let go of.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think there have been times like that, though I don’t like saying “completely sure” because I know how it sounds.

But with some customers, when they ask too many questions about the new showroom, I’ve gotten really convinced they’re coming in to check whether I’ll miss payments or whether something is wrong financially. My husband will tell me they’re probably just being friendly or curious, and I can understand that logically… but in the moment I still feel like there’s something more behind it.

I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels very real at the time.

I can tell from their face that I’m not making sense the way I meant to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… thoughts being put in or taken out, no. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. They’ll ask a lot of questions about it, and my husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right. But in the moment it feels like they’re asking for a reason. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people are talking about you or that their actions are directed at you, even if they don’t say it outright?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I know it might sound odd. But with some customers, when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just making conversation — like maybe they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to believe that, but in the moment it really feels directed at me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people are acting in a certain way toward you, like they’re watching you or trying to send you a message, even when there’s no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but sometimes with customers, especially when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I start feeling like they’re not just being polite.\n\nLike… I’ll get convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or something, because they keep asking questions. My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in person, like customers asking about the new showroom.\n\nI know it sounds odd, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but I’ve gotten convinced sometimes that they’re coming in to check whether I’m going to miss payments, because they ask so many questions. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things happening around you—like something someone said, a sound, or even events in the news—were directly connected to you in a hidden or special way, even if others wouldn’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. I mean, not really with the news or anything like that. It’s more when people are right there in front of me.\n\nLike I said, with some customers asking so many questions about the new showroom, I start to feel like there’s something behind it… like they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if things are going badly. I know that probably sounds strange, and my husband tells me they’re just being friendly or curious. I try to believe him, I really do. But in the moment, it feels connected to me in that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on the street, a comment from a stranger, or even something in the environment like a noise or a sign, was meant to send you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like signs or noises exactly… not that I can think of. It’s more with people, especially customers.\n\nI know it probably sounds odd, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but when they ask a lot about the new showroom, I can get this feeling that they’re really checking on me—like whether I’m going to miss payments or if things aren’t going well. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced by something or someone outside of yourself, like your mind isn’t fully your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like my thoughts are being controlled, exactly. That sounds stronger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more that I can get really convinced about something, even when my husband tells me I’m probably taking it the wrong way. Like with customers asking so many questions about the new showroom… I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. I know that may sound odd, and I don’t want to overstate it, but in the moment it feels very real to me. Is that along the lines of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not your own, like someone else can hear them or that they are being put into your mind?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never felt like someone could hear my thoughts, or like thoughts were being put into my head.\n\nI mean, I do get worked up about what people might mean sometimes, but my thoughts still feel like mine. I don’t want to answer it wrong, but for that specific thing, I don’t think that’s happened.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about customers checking on you or sending you messages felt completely true to you, even when others, like your husband, have given you reasons to doubt them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess in those moments it does feel true to me. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, because I know my husband may be right that they’re just being friendly, but when customers keep asking about the new showroom, I can get really convinced they’re checking on whether I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong.\n\nAfterward I can see maybe I read too much into it, but at the time it feels hard to shake. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when, despite knowing logically that your husband’s explanation made sense, you still couldn’t shake the feeling that the customers’ questions were about something more serious, like checking on our finances?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably fair to say. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you remained completely convinced about something—like customers checking on your finances—even after seeing clear evidence that it wasn’t true or after others explained it didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least at the time. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that’s happened. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people unfairly, because I know that probably isn’t right.

But when customers ask a lot of questions about the new showroom, I’ve gotten really convinced they’re coming in to see if I’m going to miss payments or if there’s trouble with the business. My husband will tell me they’re just being friendly, and I try to believe that… but sometimes I still feel sure they’re checking on me.

My husband tells me they’re probably just being friendly, and I try to take that in, but in the moment it feels like there’s a reason behind it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—are meant specifically for you, even if they don’t seem connected at first?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or songs, I don’t think. My husband tells me they’re just being friendly, and I try to accept that, but in the moment it feels like it’s meant for me somehow. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and I try to listen to that. But in the moment it can feel like their questions mean something more. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being influenced by something or someone outside of yourself, like your mind isn’t fully your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like my thoughts are being controlled, exactly. Is that along the lines of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not your own, like someone else can hear them or that they are being put into your mind?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.
